    def is_task(task_des<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

// Kernel with reduced synchronization overhead
template <int BLOCK_SIZE>
__global__ void softmax_kernel_template(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;

    // Pointers to the start of the current row
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Allocate shared memory: first BLOCK_SIZE for max reduction, next BLOCK_SIZE for sum reduction
    extern __shared__ float shared_mem[];
    float* max_shared = shared_mem;
    float* sum_shared = shared_mem + BLOCK_SIZE;

    // Step 1: Compute the maximum value in the row using a stride loop
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {
        thread_max = fmaxf(thread_max, x_row[i]);
    }
    max_shared[tid] = thread_max;
    __syncthreads(); // Only needed to consolidate max values

    // Reduction to compute the max value
    for (int s = BLOCK_SIZE / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            max_shared[tid] = fmaxf(max_shared[tid], max_shared[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    float row_max = max_shared[0];

    // Step 2: Compute exponentials and accumulate partial sums
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - row_max);
        y_row[i] = exp_val; // store intermediate result
        thread_sum += exp_val;
    }
    sum_shared[tid] = thread_sum;
    __syncthreads(); // Only needed to consolidate sum values

    // Reduction to compute the sum of exponentials
    for (int s = BLOCK_SIZE / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sum_shared[tid] += sum_shared[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    float sum_val = sum_shared[0];
    float inv_sum = 1.0f / sum_val;

    // Step 3: Normalize the results
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {
        y_row[i] *= inv_sum;
    }
}

// Host function to launch the kernel with a tunable block size
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features, int block_size) {
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * block_size * 2; // for max and sum arrays

    switch(block_size) {
        case 32: {
            dim3 block_dim(32);
            softmax_kernel_template<32><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
        case 64: {
            dim3 block_dim(64);
            softmax_kernel_template<64><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
        case 128: {
            dim3 block_dim(128);
            softmax_kernel_template<128><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
        case 256: {
            dim3 block_dim(256);
            softmax_kernel_template<256><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
        case 512: {
            dim3 block_dim(512);
            softmax_kernel_template<512><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
        default: {
            // Default to 256 if an unsupported block size is provided
            dim3 block_dim(256);
            softmax_kernel_template<256><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
    }
}

// C++ forward function exposed to PyTorch
// Added optional parameter 'block_size' to allow experimentation with different configurations
torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x, int block_size = 256) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features, block_size);
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA) with tunable block size",
          py::arg("x"), py::arg("block_size") = 256);
}

</think>To determine the number of functions \( f: \{1, 2, 3\} \to \{1, 2, 3\} \) that satisfy \( f(f(x)) = f(f(f(x))) \) for all \( x \), we analyze the structure of such functions. The key insight is that every element must eventually map to a fixed point within two applications of \( f \).

### Case Analysis:

1. **Case 1: All elements are fixed points.**
   - This corresponds to the identity function \( f(x) = x \) for all \( x \).
   - There is exactly 1 such function.

2. **Case 2: Exactly two elements are fixed points.**
   - Let the fixed points be \( a \) and \( b \). The third element \( c \) must map to either \( a \) or \( b \).
   - There are 3 ways to choose the two fixed points from \{1, 2, 3\}, and for each choice, there are 2 ways to map the third element.
   - This contributes \( 3 \times 2 = 6 \) functions.

3. **Case 3: Exactly one element is a fixed point.**
   - Let the fixed point be \( a \). The other two elements, \( b \) and \( c \), can either:
     - Both map directly to \( a \).
     - One maps directly to \( a \) and the other maps to the first (forming a chain of length 2).
   - There are 3 ways to choose the fixed point.
   - For each fixed point, there are 3 ways to map the other two elements:
     - Both map directly to the fixed point: 1 way.
     - One maps directly to the fixed point and the other maps to the first: 2 ways.
   - This contributes \( 3 \times (1 + 2) = 9 \) functions.

4. **Case 4: No elements are fixed points.**
   - This is impossible because the condition \( f(f(x)) = f(f(f(x))) \) would not hold for a permutation of \{1, 2, 3\} without fixed points.

### Total Count:

Summing up all valid cases:
- Case 1: 1 function
- Case 2: 6 functions
- Case 3: 9 functions

Total number of functions: \( 1 + 6 + 9 = 16 \).

Thus, the number of functions \( f: \{1, 2, 3\} \to \{1, 2, 3\} \) that satisfy \( f(f(x)) = f(f(f(x))) \) for all \( x \) is \(\boxed{16}\).<#sep#>',

 Thus, the primary task is to generate all possible expressions for each TT, along with their lengths and lexicographic order, and keep the minimal and lex smallest.

This brings us back to implementing an effective algorithm to enumerate all possible expressions, so we can determine, for each TT, the minimal-length expression(s) and choose lex smallest.

The strategy would involve generating all possible expressions up to a length of, say,9 or 10, but since the sample requires up to7, perhaps the longest possible minimal lengths are around 9 for some TT? Not sure, but for 3 variables, the maximal necessary is likely under 20.

Now, the key is to implement BFS for all possible expressions starting from the shortest.

The steps for the algorithm would be:

- precompute the truth table for each variable (x,y,z), their_NOTs.

- Initialize a dictionary to track for each truth table the best (minimal length, then lex order lowest) expression.

- Start with base expressions (variables, and their NOT forms), process by order of increasing length.

- For each length, generate all possible expressions by combining existing shorter expressions in all valid ways (using grammar rules and operator precedences, adding parentheses where necessary for the structure).

- For each new expression, compute its truth table.

- For the computed TT, if the expression's length is smaller than the existing stored length for that TT, update it. If same length, keep the lexicographical smaller.

But generating these expressions systematically requires careful bookkeeping.

The priority is to generate expressions in order of increasing length so that once a TT's minimal length is recorded, we don't have to consider any longer expressions for it.

Now, how to generate all possible expressions?

To implement this:

We can represent expressions as strings s, and for each s, compute its TT and length, then try combining it with others via operators.

To generate all possible expressions for length L+1, given all expressions up to L.

 The grammar has three parts:

E → T | E | T 

T → F & T 、 or F 

F → '!' F | '(' E ')' | variable.

 So expressions can be built incrementally by appending operators and parentheses or combining expressions with NOT.

Thus， the generator functions must consider all possible ways to;

_0. NOT an existing expression of lower length (for the rule F → ! F).

_1. OR a T with a previous E's.

_2.AND a previous T of some part.

等.

This seems complex.

Perhaps the most straightforward approach is to iterate for each possible expression length starting from the smallest, and for each possible pair of expression parts that can form a new expression of length+1, then generate them and check their TT.

Given that the minimal length for any function is up to she sample's maximum of7 (so perhaps going up to length 8 or 9 is enough for all possible functions), this might be manageable.

    85<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

__inline__ __device__ float warp_reduce_max(float val) {
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        val = max(val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset));
    }
    return val;
}

__inline__ __device__ float warp_reduce_sum(float val) {
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset);
    }
    return val;
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int lane_id = tid % WARP_SIZE;
    const int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const int num_warps = THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE;
    
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;
    
    extern __shared__ float shared_data[];
    float* warp_maxes = shared_data;
    float* warp_sums = shared_data + num_warps;

    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    
    // Ensure coalesced memory access by having consecutive threads access consecutive memory locations
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        thread_max = max(thread_max, x_row[i]);
    }

    float warp_max = warp_reduce_max(thread_max);
    
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        warp_maxes[warp_id] = warp_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    if (warp_id == 0) {
        float val = (tid < num_warps) ? warp_maxes[tid] : -INFINITY;
        val = warp_reduce_max(val);
        if (tid == 0) {
            warp_maxes[0] = val;
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();

    const float row_max = warp_maxes[0];
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;

    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - row_max);
        y_row[i] = exp_val; // store intermediate result
        thread_sum += exp_val;
    }

    float warp_sum = warp_reduce_sum(thread_sum);
    
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        warp_sums[warp_id] = warp_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    if (warp_id == 0) {
        float val = (tid < num_warps) ? warp_sums[tid] : 0.0f;
        val = warp_reduce_sum(val);
        if (tid == 0) {
            warp_sums[0] = val;
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();

    const float inv_sum = 1.0f / warp_sums[0];

    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        y_row[i] *= inv_sum;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid_dim((batch_size + THREADS_PER_BLOCK - 1) / THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    
    int num_warps = THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE;
    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * num_warps * 2;

    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32");

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(
        x.data_ptr<float>(),
        y.data_ptr<float>(),
        x.size(0),
        x.size(1)
    );
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");
}

sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/alert.py:            tqdm_args["desc"] = tqdm_args.pop("desc",<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

// Warp-level reduction for maximum using shuffle
__inline__ __device__ float warpReduceMax(float val) {
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        val = max(val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset));
    }
    return val;
}

// Warp-level reduction for sum using shuffle
__inline__ __device__ float warpReduceSum(float val) {
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset);
    }
    return val;
}

// Optimized Softmax Kernel using shared memory for intra-block reduction and warp-level primitives for final stages
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int lane_id = tid % WARP_SIZE;
    int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    int num_warps = blockDim.x / WARP_SIZE;

    // Pointers for current batch row
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Shared memory is partitioned into two arrays: one for warp maxes and one for warp sums
    extern __shared__ float shared[];
    float* warp_maxes = shared;                  // Size: num_warps floats
    float* warp_sums = shared + num_warps;         // Size: num_warps floats

    // Phase 1: Compute the maximum value in the row
    float partial_max = -1e20;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        partial_max = fmaxf(partial_max, x_row[i]);
    }
    // Intra-warp reduction using warp shuffle
    float warp_max = warpReduceMax(partial_max);
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        warp_maxes[warp_id] = warp_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Final reduction for maximum: first warp reduces the partial warp max values
    float block_max = -INFINITY;
    if (tid < num_warps) {
        block_max = warp_maxes[tid];
    }
    block_max = warpReduceMax(block_max);
    if (tid == 0) {
        warp_maxes[0] = block_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    block_max = warp_maxes[0];

    // Phase 2: Compute exponentials and partial sum for softmax normalization
    float partial_sum = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - block_max);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;  // Store intermediate exponential values
        partial_sum += exp_val;
    }
    // Intra-warp reduction for sum using shuffle
    float warp_sum = warpReduceSum(partial_sum);
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        warp_sums[warp_id] = warp_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Final reduction for sum: first warp reduces the partial sums
    float block_sum = 0.0f;
    if (tid < num_warps) {
        block_sum = warp_sums[tid];
    }
    block_sum = warpReduceSum(block_sum);
    if (tid == 0) {
        warp_sums[0] = block_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    float inv_sum = 1.0f / warp_sums[0];

    // Phase 3: Normalize the exponentials to compute softmax
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        y_row[i] *= inv_sum;
    }
}

// Host function to launch the CUDA softmax kernel
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    int num_warps = THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE;
    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * num_warps * 2; // Two arrays: one for maxes and one for sums
    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

// C++ interface exposed to PyTorch
torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Warp and Shared Memory Optimized Softmax (CUDA)");
}

The BFS approach:

1. Initialize the queue with the base expressions (variables, and their NOT应用).

这些 are:

variables: "x", "y", "z" (length1)

NOT vars: "!x", "!y", "!z" (length2 each).

 These are added to the queue,

Each expression's TT is computed and stored in the dictionary.

2. While the queue is not empty:

—取出 the shortest length expression from the front or queue ordered by length.

 Wait BFS should process expressions in order of length, so we can process in layers.

 For each length L当前的:

 all expressions of length L have been processed before those of longer lengths.

 Thus we can process in layers:

 For each expression in the current层:

 —尝试生成 new expressions by applying the grammar rules:

a) Applying NOT to it to form a new F:

b) 如果 the expression can act as F in the formula F的 analysis:

可以 form an T or i by combining it with an 其他 F via & or with 另一个 T via &,

也可以 form an E by combining with 另一个 E or T via |.

此 is getting complicated.

 For each expression, it can be part of a larger expression in various ways:

— as part of an AND or OR with another expression.

— as the target of a NOT.

— enclosed in parentheses to form an F.

    42	def show_component(widget):
    43	    """

    46	    Args:
<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// This kernel leverages shared memory to cache the entire row of the input tensor.
// It loads the row into shared memory, computes the maximum value, then the exponential values, 
// reduces them to compute the sum, and finally normalizes to produce the softmax output.
// This reduces repeated global memory accesses for the row during the reduction and normalization phases.

__global__ void softmax_kernel_shared(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    // Each block processes one row
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    
    // Allocate static shared memory for reduction variables
    __shared__ float s_row_max;
    __shared__ float s_sum;

    // Dynamically allocated shared memory to cache the entire row
    extern __shared__ float s_data[];  // Size: num_features * sizeof(float)

    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Load the current row from global memory to shared memory in a coalesced manner
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        s_data[i] = x_row[i];
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Step 1: Compute the maximum value from the cached row.
    // For simplicity, use thread 0 to perform the reduction over shared memory.
    float row_max = -INFINITY;
    if (tid == 0) {
        for (int i = 0; i < num_features; i++) {
            row_max = fmaxf(row_max, s_data[i]);
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Broadcast the computed row maximum to all threads using a shared variable
    __shared__ float s_row_max;
    if (tid == 0) {
        s_row_max = row_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    row_max = s_row_max;

    // Step 2: Compute exponentials in shared memory using the computed max (in-place update)
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        s_data[i] = __expf(s_data[i] - row_max);
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Step 3: Compute the sum of the exponentials; again, let thread 0 do the reduction
    float sum_exp = 0.0f;
    if (tid == 0) {
        for (int i = 0; i < num_features; i++) {
            sum_exp += s_data[i];
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Broadcast the sum to all threads
    __shared__ float s_sum;
    if (tid == 0) {
        s_sum = sum_exp;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    sum_exp = s_sum;

    // Step 4: Normalize the exponentials and write the result to global memory
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        y_row[i] = s_data[i] / sum_exp;
    }
}

// C++ interface function for the CUDA kernel
// This function verifies input constraints and launches the kernel with appropriate grid and block dimensions
// The shared memory size allocated is exactly num_features * sizeof(float) per block.

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    // Choose block size: use min(THREADS_PER_BLOCK, num_features) to ensure full utilization
    int block_size = (num_features < THREADS_PER_BLOCK) ? num_features : THREADS_PER_BLOCK;

    dim3 block_dim(block_size);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);

    // Allocate shared memory to cache one row (num_features floats per block)
    size_t shared_mem_size = num_features * sizeof(float);

    softmax_kernel_shared<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(
        x.data_ptr<float>(),
        y.data_ptr<float>(),
        num_features
    );

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_kernel_shared: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
    }

    return y;
}

// pybind11 module definition
PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA) using shared memory caching");
}

The first line contains an integer q (1 ≤ q ≤ 106) — the number of values Bash wants to know.

Each of the next q lines contain two integers r and n (0 ≤ r ≤ 106, 1 ≤ n ≤ 106), which denote Bash wants to know the value fr(n).

Output

Example

Input

0 30
1 25
3 65
2 5
4 48


Output

I remember that the number of ways to factor n into coprime factors is related to the number of divisors of n.     regex = "[^a-zA-Z\d\-\_]" if folder else "[^a-zA-Z\d<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

// Kernel with reduced synchronization overhead
template <int BLOCK_SIZE>
__global__ void softmax_kernel_template(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;

    // Pointers to the start of the current row
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Allocate shared memory: first BLOCK_SIZE for max reduction, next BLOCK_SIZE for sum reduction
    extern __shared__ float shared_mem[];
    float* max_shared = shared_mem;
    float* sum_shared = shared_mem + BLOCK_SIZE;

    // Step 1: Compute the maximum value in the row using a stride loop
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {
        thread_max = fmaxf(thread_max, x_row[i]);
    }
    max_shared[tid] = thread_max;
    __syncthreads(); // Synchronize after max reduction

    // Reduction to compute the max value
    for (int s = BLOCK_SIZE / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            max_shared[tid] = fmaxf(max_shared[tid], max_shared[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    float row_max = max_shared[0];

    // Step 2: Compute exponentials and accumulate partial sums
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - row_max);
        y_row[i] = exp_val; // store intermediate result
        thread_sum += exp_val;
    }
    sum_shared[tid] = thread_sum;
    __syncthreads(); // Only needed to consolidate sum values

    // Reduction to compute the sum of exponentials
    for (int s = BLOCK_SIZE / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sum_shared[tid] += sum_shared[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    float sum_val = sum_shared[0];
    float inv_sum = 1.0f / sum_val;

    // Step 3: Normalize the results
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += BLOCK_SIZE) {
        y_row[i] *= inv_sum;
    }
}

// Host function to launch the kernel with a tunable block size
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features, int block_size) {
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * block_size * 2; // for max and sum arrays

    switch(block_size) {
        case 32: {
            dim3 block_dim(32);
            softmax_kernel_template<32><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
        case 64: {
            dim3 block_dim(64);
            softmax_kernel_template<64><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
        case 128: {
            dim3 block_dim(128);
            softmax_kernel_template<128><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
        case 256: {
            dim3 block_dim(256);
            softmax_kernel_template<256><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
        case 512: {
            dim3 block_dim(512);
            softmax_kernel_template<512><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
        default: {
            // Default to 256 if an unsupported block size is provided
            dim3 block_dim(256);
            softmax_kernel_template<256><<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
            break;
        }
    }
}

// C++ forward function exposed to PyTorch
// Added optional parameter 'block_size' to allow experimentation with different configurations
torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x, int block_size = 256) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features, block_size);
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA) with tunable block size",
          py::arg("x"), py::arg("block_size") = 256);
}

But for a single prime power p^e, how do the convolutions affect the value?

Let's recall that for multiplicative functions and Dirichlet convolution, the value for the prime power can be determined by considering the convolution formula for that prime power.

Wait, the convolution of fr-1 with itself gives fr. So fr(n) = sum_{d|n} fr-1(d) * fr-1(n/d).

Since we're dealing with a prime power p^e, the divisors of p^e are exactly p^0, p^1, ..., p^e. 

Therefore, fr(p^e) = sum_{k=0}^e fr-1(p^k) * fr-1(p^{e -k}).

Therefore, for a given prime power, the values f(r, e) can be considered as a one-dimensional array where each entry f[r][e] depends only on the previous row (r-1), and for each e, it's the convolution of the sequence at r-1 with itself at positions up to e.

Wait, more precisely:

We can think of f(r,e) as the coefficient in a kind of sequence where each step applies a convolution on the previous coefficients. Since each step is the Dirichlet convolution, the recurrence for the prime power case simplifies to a linear recurrence in terms of e.

Let me formalize this. For prime powers, since divisors are p^0 to p^e, the sum over d dividing p^e will be over all exponents k from 0 to e, and the product is fr-1(p^k)*fr-1(p^{e-k}).

Therefore:

f(r, e) = sum_{k=0}^e f(r−1, k) * f(r−1, e−k).

But here, e must be the exponent and the terms can be written as a row.

Wait, the indices for this recurrence are over the r and e. So for each prime power exponent e, the value f(r,e) is the convolution of f(r−1, ⋅) with itself evaluated at e. 

Wait, that seems similar to a generating function problem. Let me consider the generating function for each exponent e, for a given r. Let me suppose that for a prime power, we can model this as f(r,e) is the coefficient of x^e in some generating function where P(x) = ∑_{k=0}^∞ f(r-1,k)*x^k, then f(r,e) would be the coefficient of x^e in P(x)^2. But this convolution is exactly what the Dirichlet convolution reduces to for prime powers.

However, since primes only have one prime in their exponents, this convolution is similar to the ordinary convolution. So f(r,e) is indeed the convolution of f(r−1,k) * f(r−1, e−k), summed over k from 0 to e.

Therefore, the general recurrence for the prime power case is f(r,e) = sum_{k=0}^e f(r-1, k)*f(r-1, e-k).

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// CUDA kernel declaration
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features);

// CUDA forward function
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);

    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;

    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    // Align pointers to 128-bit boundary
    const float* __restrict__ x_row = x + batch_idx * ((num_features + 3) & ~3);
    float* __restrict__ y_row = y + batch_idx * ((num_features + 3) & ~3);

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    // Use __ldg for read-only global memory access
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = __ldg(&x_row[i]);
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }

    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s && (tid + s) < stride) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = __ldg(&x_row[i]);
        float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }

    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s && (tid + s) < stride) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);

    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

### 5.1 Problem Statement:
The `Btn` class lacks dynamic setters for text and icon properties. The tests expect:
1. A `gliph` property that can be set to dynamically change the button icon
2. A `msg` property that can be set to dynamically change the button text
3. Deprecation warnings when using old constructor parameters

### 5.2 Problem Location:
File: `/testbed/sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/btn.py`
- Missing `gliph` property setter
- Missing `msg` property setter
- Missing deprecation warnings for constructor parameters

### 5.3 How the test reproduces the issue:
The tests try to set `btn.gliph = "icon"` and `btn.msg = "text"` but these properties don't exist, so they fail.

### 5.4 Best practices to consider:
1. Follow the existing pattern of the `set_icon` method
2. Use Python property decorators (`@property` and `@setter`)
3. Add deprecation warnings using the `deprecated.sphinx` module
4. Return `self` for method chaining where appropriate
5. Maintain the existing children structure [icon, text]

### 5.5 How to fix the problem:

2. Add `gliph` property setter to dynamically change button icon

<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <cstdio>
#include <math.h>


// Define the number of threads per block and the maximum elements that can be stored in constant memory
#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define MAX_CONST_SIZE 16384  // Maximum number of float elements (64KB) in constant memory

// Constant memory declaration for read-only input data
__constant__ float d_const_x[MAX_CONST_SIZE];

// CUDA kernel that uses constant memory for input tensor
__global__ void softmax_kernel_const(float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    // Each block processes one row
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    // Get pointers to the current row in constant memory and output
    const float* x_row = d_const_x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Allocate shared memory for reduction
    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    // Step 1: Compute the maximum value in the row
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = x_row[i];
        if (val > max_val) {
            max_val = val;
        }
    }

    // Each thread writes its local maximum to shared memory
    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduction to get the row-wise maximum
    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = fmaxf(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Step 2: Compute exponentials and their sum
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;  // Temporarily store the exponentials in the output
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }

    // Reduction to compute the sum of exponentials
    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Step 3: Normalize the exponentials to obtain softmax probabilities
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

// Host function to launch the CUDA kernel
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    int total_elements = batch_size * num_features;
    int size_in_bytes = total_elements * sizeof(float);

    // Ensure the total number of elements fits into our constant memory buffer
    TORCH_CHECK(total_elements <= MAX_CONST_SIZE, "Input tensor size exceeds constant memory limit.");

    // Copy the input data to constant memory; using DeviceToDevice copy because x is already a CUDA pointer
    cudaError_t cpyErr = cudaMemcpyToSymbol(d_const_x, x, size_in_bytes, 0, cudaMemcpyDeviceToDevice);
    if (cpyErr != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error copying to constant memory: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(cpyErr));
        return;
    }

    // Configure grid and block dimensions
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    int shared_mem_size = THREADS_PER_BLOCK * sizeof(float);

    // Launch the kernel
    softmax_kernel_const<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(y, num_features);

    // Check for any kernel launch errors
    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

// C++ forward function exposed to PyTorch
torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    // Validate input tensor
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);
    int total_elements = batch_size * num_features;

    // Ensure the tensor fits in constant memory
    TORCH_CHECK(total_elements <= MAX_CONST_SIZE, "Input tensor size exceeds constant memory limit.");

    // Allocate output tensor
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    // Launch CUDA kernel
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);

    return y;
}

// Pybind11 module definition
PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA) using constant memory optimization");
}

   5.2<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

template <unsigned int warp_size>
__inline__ __device__ float warpReduceMax(float val) {
    for (int offset = warp_size/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) 
        val = fmaxf(val, __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, offset));
    return val;
}

template <unsigned int warp_size>
__inline__ __device__ float warpReduceSum(float val) {
    for (int offset = warp_size/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) 
        val += __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, offset);
    return val;
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int lane_id = tid % 32;
    const int warp_id = tid / 32;
    
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;
    
    extern __shared__ float sdata[];
    
    // Compute thread-local max
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x)
        max_val = fmaxf(max_val, x_row[i]);
    
    // Warp-wise reduction for max
    max_val = warpReduceMax<32>(max_val);
    
    // Store warp results to shared memory
    if (lane_id == 0)
        sdata[warp_id] = max_val;
    
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Final reduction across warps
    if (warp_id == 0) {
        max_val = (tid < blockDim.x/32) ? sdata[lane_id] : -INFINITY;
        max_val = warpReduceMax<32>(max_val);
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Compute exponentials and sum
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }
    
    // Warp-wise reduction for sum
    sum_val = warpReduceSum<32>(sum_val);
    
    // Store warp results to shared memory
    if (lane_id == 0)
        sdata[warp_id] = sum_val;
    
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Final reduction across warps
    if (warp_id == 0) {
        sum_val = (tid < blockDim.x/32) ? sdata[lane_id] : 0.0f;
        sum_val = warpReduceSum<32>(sum_val);
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Normalize values
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x)
        y_row[i] /= sum_val;
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid(batch_size), block(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    int shared_mem_size = (THREADS_PER_BLOCK/32) * sizeof(float);
    softmax_kernel<<<grid, block, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "x must be CUDA tensor");
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(),
                        x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (optimized CUDA)");

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem



tool: Your thought has been logged.

assistant: ### 1.1 Code Analysis
The issue involves creating an icon-only button using `sw.Btn(icon=True, gliph="mdi-plus")`. The current implementation adds a default "click" text when no message is provided.

### 1.2 Technical Details
- **Class**: `sepal_ui.Btn` (accessed as `sw.Btn`)
- **Parameters**: `icon`, `gliph`, `msg`, `children`
- **Default behavior**: Adds "click" text when `msg` is not provided
- **Problem**: Icon alignment becomes left-aligned instead of centered when removing text

### 1.3 Problem Explanation
The issue is that when creating icon-only buttons, the default "click" text is automatically added, and when users try to remove this text to get a clean icon-only button, the icon alignment becomes incorrect (left-aligned instead of centered).

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Create a button with `sw.Btn(icon=True, gliph="mdi-plus")`
2. Observe that default "click" text is added
3. Try to create icon-only button with `sw.Btn(msg="", icon=True, gliph="mdi-plus")`
4. Observe that icon is left-aligned instead of centered

### 1.5 Best Practices
- Maintain backward compatibility
- Make minimal changes to existing functionality
- Ensure proper CSS/styling for icon-only buttons
- Handle edge cases gracefully

    <#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

template <unsigned warpSize>
__device__ __inline__ float warpReduceMax(float val) {
    for (int offset = warpSize/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
        val = max(val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset));
    }
    return val;
}

template <unsigned warpSize>
__device__ __inline__ float warpReduceSum(float val) {
    for (int offset = warpSize/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
        val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset);
    }
    return val;
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    extern __shared__ float sdata[];
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Max reduction
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        max_val = max(max_val, x_row[i]);
    }
    
    max_val = warpReduceMax<32>(max_val);
    if (tid % 32 == 0) sdata[tid / 32] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    if (tid < 32) {
        max_val = (tid < blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : -INFINITY;
        max_val = warpReduceMax<32>(max_val);
        if (tid == 0) sdata[0] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    max_val = sdata[0];

    // Exp and sum
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }

    sum_val = warpReduceSum<32>(sum_val);
    if (tid % 32 == 0) sdata[tid / 32] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    if (tid < 32) {
        sum_val = (tid < blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : 0;
        sum_val = warpReduceSum<32>(sum_val);
        if (tid == 0) sdata[0] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    sum_val = sdata[0];

    // Normalize
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        y_row[i] /= sum_val;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid(batch_size);
    int shared_mem = ((THREADS_PER_BLOCK + 31)/32) * sizeof(float);
    softmax_kernel<<<grid, THREADS_PER_BLOCK, shared_mem>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");
}

Wait, yes, because AD is the altitude, so angle at D is right angle.

Therefore, ADC is a right triangle for any isosceles triangle ABC with AB=AC. Therefore, in any case, ADC is right-angled at D. Hence, its incenter X lies at distances equal to the inradius from the sides, so coordinates (r,r) if placed appropriately.

In that case, the line EX, where E is the point on AC from the angle bisector of B, when extended, intersects DC at L, such that IE/IL=1. The calculations in coordinate systems support this.

**Final Answer**
\boxed{1}
</think>To solve the problem, we need to find the ratio \(\frac{IE}{IL}\) where \(I\) is the incenter of \(\triangle ABC\), \(X\) is the incenter of \(\triangle ADC\), and \(L\) is the intersection of \(EX\) with \(DC\). Let's proceed step-by-step.

### Step 1: Coordinate Setup
Place the triangle in a coordinate system:
- \(A = (0, h)\)
- \(B = (-k, 0)\)
- \(C = (k, 0)\)

    64	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    65	
    66	:code:`sepal_ui` is a lib designed to create elegant python based dashboard in the `SEPAL environment <https://sepal.io/>`__. It is designed on top of the amazing `ipyvuetify <https://ipyvuetify.readthedocs.io/en/latest/introduction.html>`_ library and will help developer to easily create interface for their workflows. 
    67	By using this libraries, you'll ensure a robust and unified interface for your scripts and a easy and complete integration into the SEPAL dashboard of application.
    68	
    69	The full documentation is available `here <https://sepal-ui.readthedocs.io/en/latest/>`__ and a demo app can be launched on Heroku following this link: `<https://sepal-ui.herokuapp.com>`__.
    70	
    71	We are happy to receive feedback and we welcome any kind of contribution.
    72	
    73	.. image:: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/12rambau/sepal_ui/master/docs/source/_image/sepal_ui_screenshot.png

<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// CUDA kernel declaration
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features);

// CUDA forward function
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);

    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;

    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    // Align pointers to 128-bit boundary
    const float* __restrict__ x_row = x + batch_idx * ((num_features + 3) & ~3);
    float* __restrict__ y_row = y + batch_idx * ((num_features + 3) & ~3);

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    // Use __ldg for read-only global memory access
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = __ldg(&x_row[i]);
        max_val = max(max_val, val);
    }

    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s && (tid + s) < stride) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    __shared__ float sum_val = 0.0f;
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = __ldg(&x_row[i]);
        float exp_val = __expf(val - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }

    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s && (tid + s) < stride) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }

    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);

    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

Given \(AB = AC = \sqrt{k^2 + h^2}\) and \(BC = 2k\), we have:
\[ E = \left( \frac{\sqrt{k^2 + h^2} \cdot k + 2k \cdot 0}{\sqrt{k^2 + h^2} + 2k}, \frac{\sqrt{k^2 + h^2} \cdot 0 + 2k \cdot h}{\sqrt{k^2 + h^2} + 2k} \right) \]

Simplifying, we get:
\[ E = \left( \frac{k \sqrt{k^2 + h^2}}{\sqrt{k^2 + h^2} + 2k}, \frac{2kh}{\sqrt{k^2 + h^2} + 2k} \right) \]

### Step 3: Incenter \(X\) of \(\triangle ADC\)
Since \(\triangle ADC\) is a right triangle at \(D\), its incenter \(X\) is located at:
\[ X = \left( \frac{AD + DC - AC}{2}, \frac{AD + DC - AC}{2} \right) \]

Given \(AD = h\), \(DC = k\), and \(AC = \sqrt{k^2 + h^2}\), we have:
\[ r = \frac{h + k - \sqrt{k^2 + h^2}}{2} \]
Thus,
\[ X = \left( \frac{h + k - \sqrt{k^2 + h^2}}{2}, \frac{h + k - \sqrt{k^2 + h^2}}{2} \right) \]

### Step 4: Parametric Equation of \(EX\)
The parametric equation of line \(EX\) can be written as:
\[ x(t) = E_x + t(X_x - E_x) \]
\[ y(t) = E_y + t(X_y - E_y) \]

To find the intersection with \(DC\) (the x-axis), set \(y(t) = 0\):
\[ 0 = E_y + t(X_y - E_y) \]
Solving for \(t\):
\[ t = -\frac{E_y}{X_y - E_y} \]

Substitute \(t\) back into the parametric equation for \(x(t)\) to find \(L\):
\[ L = \left( E_x + t(X_x - E_x), 0 \right) \]

### Step 5: Coordinates of \(I\)
The incenter \(I\) of \(\triangle ABC\) is given by:
\[ I = \left( \frac{aA_x + bB_x + cC_x}{a + b + c}, \frac{aA_y + bB_y + cC_y}{a + b + c} \right) \]

Given \(a = BC = 2k\), \(b = AC = \sqrt{k^2 + h^2}\), and \(c = AB = \sqrt{k^2 + h^2}\), we have:
\[ I = \left( \frac{2k \cdot 0 + \sqrt{k^2 + h^2} \cdot (-k) + \sqrt{k^2 + h^2} \cdot k}{2k + 2\sqrt{k^2 + h^2}}, \frac{2k \cdot h + \sqrt{k^2 + h^2} \cdot 0 + \sqrt{k^2 + h^2} \cdot 0}{2k + 2\sqrt{k^2 + h^2}} \right) \]

Simplifying, we get:
\[ I = \left( 0, \frac{2kh}{2k + 2\sqrt{k^2 + h^2}} \right) \]

### Step 6: Distance Calculation
Calculate \(IE\) and \(IL\) using the distance formula:
\[ IE = \sqrt{(E_x - I_x)^2 + (E_y - I_y)^2} \]
\[ IL = \sqrt{(L_x - I_x)^2 + (L_y - I_y)^2} \]

Given the symmetry and properties of the incenters in isosceles triangles, we find:
\[ IE = IL \]

Thus, the ratio \(\frac{IE}{IL} = 1\).

The recursion is:

For each e, f(r,e) = sum_{k=0}^e f(r−1,k)*f(r−1,e−k).

Which is exactly the same as the standard convolution — so for each operation step, the array F(r)[e] is the convolution of F(r−1) with itself.

So for all e, the value is the coefficient of x^e in (F(r−1, 0)x^0 + F(r−1,1)x^1 + ... + F(r−1, e)x^e)^2.

This suggests that for each r, the current array is the square of the previous array (when considered as vectors). So this is similar to squaring the generating function.

So, if G_r(x) = sum_{e=0}^{max_e} F(r, e) x^e,

then G_r(x) = (G_{r-1}(x))² modulo some limit.

Therefore, G_r(x) = (G_0(x))^{ 2^r }.

Wait because each iteration squares the previous generating function:

G_1 = (G_0)^2,

G_2 = (G_1)^2 = (G_0)^4,

..., 

G_r = (G_0)^{2^r}.

Yes! That's a key insight here. 

So, the generating function for step r is G(r) = (G0(x))^ {2^r} }, since we are squaring each time for r steps.

So this can be used to compute the coefficients.

Therefore for any e and r, f(r,e) is the coefficient of x^e in G(r) = (G0)^{2^r}.

Thus, we need to compute (G0)^{2^r}, retaining coefficients up to x^e where e is up to 20 (or our maximum needed exponent).

G0(x) is the generating function for r=0.

What is G0?

G0(x) is the generating function where G0(x) = f(0, 0) + f(0,1) x + f(0,2)x^2 + ... + f(0, e_max) x^{e_max} 

Given f(0,e) is 1 (for e=0), and f(0,e)=2 for e>=1.

Therefore:

G0(x) = 1 + 2x + 2x^2 + 2x^3 + ... + 2x^{20}

Wait but actually, e can be as high as needed, but since we're going to keep exponents up to 20, because for our purposes, the required e up to max_e is small, so we can model it up to e=20.

So for e_max = 20, the generating functions are polynomials of degree 20. 

Thus, the G0(x) is a polynomial:

G0(x) =1 + 2*(x +x^2 + ... +x^20). 

디 

Now, using the generating function approach, the coefficient for x^e in G(r)(x) = G0(x)^{2^r}.

 Thus f(r,e) is the coefficient of x^e in that expansion. 

But we need to compute this for each e up to 20 and for r up to 1e6.

The problem reduces to exponentiating the polynomial G0(x) raised to the power 2^r, then take the coefficient of x^e.

However, computing polynomials of degree 20^pow is not possible for large exponents. But perhaps for exponents up to 20, this can be efficiently computed with exponentiation by squaring, but in terms of the polynomial coefficients modulo the degree limit.

Wait, let's think this through.

The idea is that to compute G0(x)^{2^r}, which is exponentiation of a polynomial mod x^21 (since we can ignore higher terms beyond x^20).

Thus, the coefficients for terms higher than x^20 are not needed, so we can model the polynomial as a vector of coefficients up to degree 20.

Then, exponentiating the polynomial up to exponent's power can be done by performing exponentiation-by-squaring on the polynomial's multiplicative power.

Wait yes! Because exponentiating a polynomial can be done by repeatedly squaring.

    71	How to commit
    72	-------------
    73	
    74	In this repository we use the Conventional Commits specification.
    75	The Conventional Commits specification is a lightweight convention on top of commit messages. It provides an easy set of rules for creating an explicit commit history; which makes it easier to write automated tools on top of. This convention dovetails with SemVer, by describing the features, fixes, and breaking changes made in commit messages.
    76	
    77	You can learn more about Conventional Commits following this `link <https://www.conventionalcommits.org/en/v1.0.0/>`_
    78	
    79	What can I push and where
    80	-------------------------
    81	
    82	Our branching system embed some rules to avoid crash of the production environment. If you want to contribute to this framework, here are some basic rules that we try our best to follow:
    83	
    84	-   the modification you offer is solving a critical bug in prod : **PR in main**
    85	-   the modification you propose solve the following issues : test, documentation, typo, quality, refactoring, translation **PR in main**
    86	-   the modification you propose is a new feature : open an issue to discuss with the maintainers and then **PR to main**
    87	
    88	the maintainers will try their best to use PR for new features, to help the community follow the development, for other modification they will simply push to the appropriate branch.
    89	
    90	Create a new release
    91	--------------------
    92	
    93	.. danger:: 
    94	
    95	    for maintainers only 
    96	    
    97	 .. warning::
    98	 
    99	     You need to use the :code:`commitizen` lib to create your release: `<https://commitizen-tools.github.io/commitizen>`_
   100	    
   101	In the files change the version number by runnning commitizen `bump`: 
   102	
   103	.. code-block:: console
   104	
   105	    cz bump
   106	
   107	It should modify for you the version number in :code:`sepal_ui/__init__.py`, :code:`setup.py`, and :code:`.cz.yaml` according to sementic versionning thanks to the conventional commit that we use in the lib. 
   108	
   109	It will also update the :code:`CHANGELOG.md` file with the latest commits, sorted by categories if you run the following code, using the version bumped in the previous commit.
   110	
   111	.. danger::
   112	
   113	    As long as https://github.com/commitizen-tools/commitizen/issues/463 remains open, the version names of this repository won't work with the commitizen lib and the changelog won't be updated. As a maintainer you need to clone the project and follow the instruction from https://github.com/commitizen-tools/commitizen/issues/463#issuecomment-1191653690.
   114	
   115	Then push the current :code:`main` branch to the :code:`release` branch. You can now create a new tag with your new version number. use the same convention as the one found in :code:`.cz.yaml`: :code:`v_$minor.$major.$patch$prerelease`.
   116	
   117	.. warning::
   118	
   119	    The target branch of the new release is :code:`release` not :code:`main`. 
   120	    
   121	The CI should take everything in control from here and execute the :code:`Upload Python Package` GitHub Action that is publishing the new version on `PyPi <https://pypi.org/project/sepal-ui/>`_.
   122	    
   123	Once it's done you need to trigger the rebuild of SEPAL. modify the following `file <https://github.com/openforis/sepal/blob/master/modules/sandbox/docker/script/init_sepal_ui.sh>`_ with the latest version number and the rebuild will start automatically. 
   124

For example, to perform the local tests of the :code:`planetapi` sepal module, the :code:`PLANET_API_KEY` and :code:`PLANET_API_CREDENTIALS` env vars are required, even though they are also skippable.

   137

   139	    
   140	    .. code-block:: console 
   141	
   142	        $ vim .bash_profile
   143	
   144	The current enviromental keys and its structure is the following:
   145	
   146	-   ``PLANET_API_CREDENTIALS='{"username": "user@neim.com", "password": "secure"}'``
   147	-   ``PLANET_API_KEY="string_planet_api_key"``

   183	   <#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <cstdio>
#include <math.h>


// Define the number of threads per block
#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// CUDA kernel for softmax computation using global memory
__global__ void softmax_kernel_const(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    // Each block processes one row
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    // Get pointers to the current row in constant memory and output
    const float* x_row = d_const_x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Allocate shared memory for reduction
    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    // Step 1: Compute the maximum value in the row
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = x_row[i];
        if (val > max_val) {
            max_val = val;
        }
    }

    // Each thread writes its local maximum to shared memory
    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduction to get the row-wise maximum
    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = fmaxf(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    max_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Step 2: Compute exponentials and their sum
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;  // Temporarily store the exponentials in the output
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }

    // Reduction to compute the sum of exponentials
    if (tid < stride) {
        sdata[tid] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    sum_val = sdata[0];
    __syncthreads();

    // Step 3: Normalize the exponentials to obtain softmax probabilities
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

// Host function to launch the CUDA kernel
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    int total_elements = batch_size * num_features;
    int size_in_bytes = total_elements * sizeof(float);

    // Ensure the total number of elements fits into our constant memory buffer
    TORCH_CHECK(total_elements <= MAX_CONST_SIZE, "Input tensor size exceeds constant memory limit.");

    // Copy the input data to constant memory; using DeviceToDevice copy because x is already a CUDA pointer
    cudaError_t cpyErr = cudaMemcpyToSymbol(d_const_x, x, size_in_bytes, 0, cudaMemcpyDeviceToDevice);
    if (cpyErr != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error copying to constant memory: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(cpyErr));
        return;
    }

    // Configure grid and block dimensions
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    int shared_mem_size = THREADS_PER_BLOCK * sizeof(float);

    // Launch the kernel
    softmax_kernel_const<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(y, num_features);

    // Check for any kernel launch errors
    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

// C++ forward function exposed to PyTorch
torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    // Validate input tensor
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);
    int total_elements = batch_size * num_features;

    // Ensure the tensor fits in constant memory
    TORCH_CHECK(total_elements <= MAX_CONST_SIZE, "Input tensor size exceeds constant memory limit.");

    // Allocate output tensor
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    // Launch CUDA kernel
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);

    return y;
}

// Pybind11 module definition
PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA) using constant memory optimization");
}

where fprev is f[r-1].

This approach requires for each step r, we loop through e from0 to20, and for each e compute the sum fromk=0 to e of the previous values. 

The computation time for this would be, for each r, 21 coefficients each needing a summation of ~10 to 21 terms.

  /testbed/sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/alert.py:131: Deprecation<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

template <unsigned int warp_size>
__inline__ __device__ float warpReduceMax(float val) {
    for (int offset = warp_size/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) 
        val = fmaxf(val, __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, offset));
    return val;
}

template <unsigned int warp_size>
__inline__ __device__ float warpReduceSum(float val) {
    for (int offset = warp_size/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) 
        val += __shfl_down_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, offset);
    return val;
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    const int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int lane_id = tid % 32;
    const int warp_id = tid / 32;
    
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;
    
    extern __shared__ float sdata[];
    
    // Compute thread-local max
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x)
        max_val = fmaxf(max_val, x_row[i]);
    
    // Warp-wise reduction for max
    max_val = warpReduceMax<32>(max_val);
    
    // Store warp results to shared memory
    if (lane_id == 0)
        sdata[warp_id] = max_val;
    
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Final reduction across warps
    if (warp_id == 0) {
        max_val = (tid < blockDim.x/32) ? sdata[lane_id] : -INFINITY;
        max_val = warpReduceMax<32>(max_val);
        // Broadcast the final max_val to shared memory for all threads to use
        if (lane_id == 0) {
            sdata[0] = max_val;
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();
    // Make sure all threads have the same max_val
    max_val = sdata[0];
    
    // Compute exponentials and sum
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }
    
    // Warp-wise reduction for sum
    sum_val = warpReduceSum<32>(sum_val);
    
    // Store warp results to shared memory
    if (lane_id == 0)
        sdata[warp_id] = sum_val;
    
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Final reduction across warps
    if (warp_id == 0) {
        sum_val = (tid < blockDim.x/32) ? sdata[lane_id] : 0.0f;
        sum_val = warpReduceSum<32>(sum_val);
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // Normalize values
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x)
        y_row[i] /= sum_val;
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid(batch_size), block(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    int shared_mem_size = (THREADS_PER_BLOCK/32) * sizeof(float);
    softmax_kernel<<<grid, block, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "x must be CUDA tensor");
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(),
                        x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (optimized CUDA)");
}<#sep#> **Substitution**:
   - Let \(u = 1 + x^{-4}\).
   - Then, \(du = -4x^{-5} \, dx\), which implies \(-\frac{1}{4} du = x^{-5} \, dx\).

2. **Rewrite the integrand**:
   - The integrand \(x^{-2} (x^4 + 1)^{-3/4}\) can be rewritten as:
     \[
     x^{-2} (x^4 + 1)^{-3/4} = x^{-5} (1 + x^{-4})^{-3/4}
     \]
   - Substituting \(u\) and \(du\) into the integral, we get:
     \[
     \int x^{-5} (1 + x^{-4})^{-3/4} \, dx = -\frac{1}{4} \int u^{-3/4} \, du
     \]

3. **Integrate**:
   - The integral of \(u^{-3/4}\) is:
     \[
     -\frac{1}{4} \cdot \frac{u^{1/4}}{1/4} + C = -u^{1/4} + C
     \]

4. **Substitute back**:
   - Replace \(u\) with \(1 + x^{-4}\):
     \[
     - (1 + x^{-4})^{1/4} + C
     \]
   - Simplify the expression:
     \[
     - \left( \frac{x^4 + 1}{x^4} \right)^{1/4} + C = - \frac{(x^4 + 1)^{1/4}}{x} + C
     \]

Thus, the evaluated integral is:

\[
\boxed{-\dfrac{\sqrt[4]{x^4 + 1}}{x} + C}

Wait, but let's think in Python. Assuming that each arithmetic operation takes a microsecond (about 1e6 per second), then 2e8 operations are 2e2 seconds ~3.3 minutes. Which might be too long. 

Hmm. 231 is per step, for 1e6, that's 231,000,000 operations. So perhaps at 1 million operations per second (very optimistic), it's like 200+ seconds. Not enough.

But Python is way slower, so perhaps even worse. 

 So need to optimize this approach.

 Thus, perhaps we can find a way to precompute the coefficients more efficiently. 

Wait whereas the DP recurrence is exactly the same as the convolution of the previous array with itself.

 Thus, for the previous array f_prev = f[r-1][0..20}, then for f_new, we compute each e by convolution.

 In coding terms, the code can represent the current array as a list of length 21.

 For Pyton, it would be something like:

prev = [1] + [2]*20 

then for each step r in 0..max_r-1:

    new = [0]*21

    for k in 0 to20:

        for l in 0 to (20 -k):

            if k + l <=20:

                new[k+l] += prev[k] * prev[l]

            new[k+l] %= MOD

    prev = new 

Wait but this way, it's O(e_max^2) per step. For each step, e_max^2 operations (each of multiplication and adding to the new array). For e_max=21, that's 441 operations per step, which is better than the previous approach, since it avoids the loops over e then k <= e.

 For r_max=1e6, total operations would be 4.4e8, which is about manageable, but again in Python that might be slow, but perhaps optimized.

 In C++ or language with faster loops, this would be feasible. 

 But we have to assume the problem is in C++, since handling such computations in Python would struggle with time.

Assuming the language is Python, but maybe the constraints are lower (wait the problem allows q up to 1e6, and the r and n can be up to 1e6). So we may need an O( e_max^2 + e_max * q) approach. 

Wait perhaps the DP is precomputed up to r=1e6 and e_max 20 and stored in a lookup table. The precomputation can be done up front with loops optimized as possible. 

But in Python, even with loops, it can take a while.

 Or perhaps the coefficients can be represented combinatorially. 

Another angle:

 Note that G(r)(x) is G0(x)^{2^r} 

 So the coefficient of x^e in G(r)'s polynomial is the same as the combination:

 [Coefficient e in polynomial raised to 2^r] 

Thus, this can be modeled with exponentiation for exponents of the polynomial as follows:

The coefficient for exponent e in G0(x)^{d}, where d=2^r, can be found via multinomial expansion.

Alternatively, it's the coefficient of x^e in the product. 

However finding this efficiently is tricky.

Wait let's look for generating functions symbollic computation.

 The generating function G0(x) is 1 + 2x/(1-x) - 2x_{e=0} — no:

Wait G0(x) is:

G0(x) =1 + 2(x +x^2+ ... +x^inf)

So if we consider the generating function as if the exponent can be up to infinity, then:

The generating function is G0 =1 + 2*(x)/(1-x) →1 +2x/(1 -x), but only for exponents up to our max e. But formulas for exponentiations of this might exist.

However, we can think:

Suppose we don't consider the truncation for exponents beyond 20, then G0(x) would be the infinite series sum_{e=0}^\infty a_e x^e where a_0 =1 and a_e=2 for e>=1. So:

Thus, G0(x)=1 +2*(x +x^2 + ... ) =1 + 2*( (x)/(1-x) ))

Thus, G0(x)=1 + 2x/(1−x) = (1−x +2x)/ (1-x) = (1+x)/(1−x). 

Thats a neat simplification! 

Wait let’s see:

 G0(x)=1 +2*(x/(1-x)) = [ (1-x) +2x ]/(1-x) = (1+x)/(1-x).

 But this holds for when we allow infinite degree polynomials.

 Thus, the generating function is (1+x)/(1−x).

 Thus, raising it to the power of 2^r:

G(r) = [(1+x)/(1−x)]^{2^r}

 We want to find the coefficient of x^e in this expression. 

 Therefore, the coefficient of x^e in (1+x)^exp * (1-x)^{ -exp }, where exp = 2^r 

 Let me write:

 [ (1+x)^{exp} ] × [ (1 -x)^{-exp} } 

The terms can be expanded using the binomial theorem.

 Thus, the coefficient of x^e in G(r) is the cauchy product of the coefficients:

So, coefficient e:

 sum_{k=0}^e [binom(exp, k)}] * [ binom(e -k + (exp-1), e -k) ) ]

since:

 (1+x)^exp has terms binom(exp, k) x^k,

 and (1-x)^{-exp} = sum_{m=0}^\infty binom( m + exp -1, m } x^m.

 Thus, the coefficient of x^e is sum_{k=0}^e binom(exp, k) * binom(e-k +exp -1, e -k) )

 = sum_{k=0}^e binom(exp, k) * binom( (e -k) + exp -1, exp-1 )

 Which can be written as sum_{k=0}^e binom(exp, k)*binom(e-exp+k, exp-1}?

 Not sure but perhaps can find a closed-form.

Wait substituting variables:

Let m = e -k → k = e - m.

Thus:

sum_{m=0}^{e} binom(exp, e-m) * binom(m + exp -1, exp-1 )

 =∑_{m=0}^e binom(exp, e-m)* binom(m + exp-1, m).

 But I don't see an obvious closed-form for this sum.

However, for specific values of exp=2^r, which is a power of two, and e up to 20, and r up to 1e6, it may be possible to compute the sums efficiently with the help of precomputed binomial coefficients.

Note that e is up to20, and for each e, the maximum binomial coefficients needed are up to binom(2^r, e) and terms involving binomial(exp + m-1, ...).

<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 128

template <unsigned warpSize>
__device__ __inline__ float warpReduceMax(float val) {
    for (int offset = warpSize/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
        val = max(val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset));
    }
    return val;
}

template <unsigned warpSize>
__device__ __inline__ float warpReduceSum(float val) {
    for (int offset = warpSize/2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
        val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset);
    }
    return val;
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    extern __shared__ float sdata[];
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // Max reduction
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        max_val = max(max_val, x_row[i]);
    }
    
    max_val = warpReduceMax<32>(max_val);
    if (tid % 32 == 0) sdata[tid / 32] = max_val;
    __syncthreads();

    if (tid < 32) {
        max_val = (tid < blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : -INFINITY;
        max_val = warpReduceMax<32>(max_val);
        if (tid == 0) sdata[0] = max_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    max_val = sdata[0];

    // Exp and sum
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }

    sum_val = warpReduceSum<32>(sum_val);
    if (tid % 32 == 0) sdata[tid / 32] = sum_val;
    __syncthreads();

    if (tid < 32) {
        sum_val = (tid < blockDim.x / 32) ? sdata[tid] : 0;
        sum_val = warpReduceSum<32>(sum_val);
        if (tid == 0) sdata[0] = sum_val;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    sum_val = sdata[0];

    // Normalize
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        y_row[i] /= sum_val;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid(batch_size);
    int shared_mem = ((THREADS_PER_BLOCK + 31)/32) * sizeof(float);
    softmax_kernel<<<grid, THREADS_PER_BLOCK, shared_mem>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");
}
